The OpenMP build reproduces the original program's output exactly, including the `-r` hash, across thread counts from 1 to 256.\n\n**Changes (only `nbody/nbody.cpp` and `nbody/CMakeLists.txt` were edited):**\n- **`computeForces`**: bodies are processed in blocks of 4 under `#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)`. The four bodies in a block are computed together so the compiler can vectorize across bodies. Each body still sums over `j` in the original order, so there is no reassociation.\n- **`integrateBodies`**: parallel loop over bodies.\n- **Left serial on purpose**: `randomizeBodies` (it uses `rand_r`, and the sequence must be preserved), and `computeTotalEnergy`, which only runs with `-v`. Parallelizing the energy sum would change its summation order.\n- **CMake**: `find_package(OpenMP REQUIRED)`, linked with `OpenMP::OpenMP_CXX`, so the build fails if OpenMP is missing. I also added `-ffp-contract=off`.\n\n**Why `-ffp-contract=off`:** With default contraction, GCC fused multiply-adds differently once the loop was outlined by OpenMP, which changed the last bits of the results. Disabling contraction makes the parallel build match the serial semantics exactly. In an earlier measurement on the scalar kernel, the cost was about 2%.\n\n**Verification:** I built the original with the same flags as a reference and compared outputs for sizes 0, 1, 2, 3, 7, 1000, 1023, 2048, and 4096 (including tail-block and zero-step cases), at 1, 3, 16, and 256 threads. All matched byte-for-byte except the timing line.\n\n**Performance** (`-n 8192 -s 5`, this box is 2×64 cores with SMT):\n\n| Build | Time |\n|---|---|\n| Original | 2480 ms |\n| New, 1 thread | 646 ms |\n| New, 32 threads | 27 ms |\n| New, 64–128 threads | ~19–20 ms |\n\nAt 256 threads (all SMT siblings) this kernel gets slower, so the default OpenMP thread count is not always best. Use `OMP_NUM_THREADS` to tune it. I did not hard-code a count.\n\nNo new files were created in the repo. `output.txt` was already in the repo and I did not modify it.
